=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two integer arrays, nums and cost, of the same size, and an integer k.
You can divide nums into subarrays. The cost of the i^th subarray consisting of elements nums[l..r] is:

(nums[0] + nums[1] + ... + nums[r] + k * i) * (cost[l] + cost[l + 1] + ... + cost[r]).

Note that i represents the order of the subarray: 1 for the first subarray, 2 for the second, and so on.
Return the minimum total cost possible from any valid division.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [3,1,4], cost = [4,6,6], k = 1
Output: 110
Explanation:
The minimum total cost possible can be achieved by dividing nums into subarrays [3, 1] and [4].


The cost of the first subarray [3,1] is (3 + 1 + 1 * 1) * (4 + 6) = 50.
The cost of the second subarray [4] is (3 + 1 + 4 + 1 * 2) * 6 = 60.


Example 2:

Input: nums = [4,8,5,1,14,2,2,12,1], cost = [7,2,8,4,2,2,1,1,2], k = 7
Output: 985
Explanation:
The minimum total cost possible can be achieved by dividing nums into subarrays [4, 8, 5, 1], [14, 2, 2], and [12, 1].


The cost of the first subarray [4, 8, 5, 1] is (4 + 8 + 5 + 1 + 7 * 1) * (7 + 2 + 8 + 4) = 525.
The cost of the second subarray [14, 2, 2] is (4 + 8 + 5 + 1 + 14 + 2 + 2 + 7 * 2) * (2 + 2 + 1) = 250.
The cost of the third subarray [12, 1] is (4 + 8 + 5 + 1 + 14 + 2 + 2 + 12 + 1 + 7 * 3) * (1 + 2) = 210.


 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 1000
cost.length == nums.length
1 <= nums[i], cost[i] <= 1000
1 <= k <= 1000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def minimumCost(self, nums: List[int], cost: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Cost of i-th subarray (1-indexed) covering l..r is (prefix sum nums up to r + k*i) * (sum cost l..r). Wait formula says (nums[0]+...+nums[r] + k*i) * (cost[l]+...+cost[r]). It uses total sum from beginning to r, not sum of subarray. Important. In examples first subarray [3,1] l=0 r=1: prefix nums up to r=4 + k*1 =5 times cost 10=50. Second [4] l=2 r=2: prefix nums up to r=8 + k*2=10 times cost 6=60. Yes.

Need minimize sum over segments: for segment [l,r] at position i (1-based), contribution = (P[r] + k*i) * C(l,r), where P[r]=sum nums[0..r], C(l,r)=sum cost[l..r]. Need partition [0,n-1].

Let prefix P[0..n] maybe P[i]=sum nums[0..i-1], P[0]=0. Segment from l to r (0-based) has prefix ending P[r+1]? Formula nums[0]+...+nums[r] = P[r+1]. Let's define prefN[j]=sum nums[0..j-1], j=0..n. Segment [l..r] corresponds start index a=l, end b=r+1 (exclusive), prefix sum up to end = prefN[b]. Cost = (prefN[b] + k*i) * (prefC[b]-prefC[a]) where prefC[j]=sum cost[0..j-1].

Need partition sequence of cut positions 0 = x0 < x1 < ... < xm = n. Segment t (1-based) from x_{t-1} to x_t-1? Actually segment t covers positions x_{t-1}..x_t-1, end exclusive x_t. Cost_t = (prefN[x_t] + k*t) * (prefC[x_t]-prefC[x_{t-1}]).

Total = sum_{t=1..m} (prefN[x_t] + k*t) * ΔC_t, where ΔC_t = prefC[x_t]-prefC[x_{t-1}].

Need minimize over m and cuts.

Define class or functions:

def value(line, x):
    m,b = line
    return m*x + b

def is_bad(l1,l2,l3):
    # slopes m1 > m2 > m3
    # remove l2 if intersection(l1,l2) >= intersection(l2,l3)
    # (b2-b1)/(m1-m2) >= (b3-b2)/(m2-m3)
    return (b2 - b1) * (m2 - m3) >= (b3 - b2) * (m1 - m2)

But wait if m2-m3 positive? Since m2 > m3, yes. m1-m2 positive. Good.

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minimumCost(self, nums: List[int], cost: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        prefN = [0]*(n+1)
        prefC = [0]*(n+1)
        for i in range(n):
            prefN[i+1] = prefN[i] + nums[i]
            prefC[i+1] = prefC[i] + cost[i]
        INF = 10**30
        dp_prev = [INF]*(n+1)
        dp_prev[0] = 0
        ans = INF

        # maybe define local variables for speed
        A = prefN
        B = prefC

        for t in range(1, n+1):
            dp_cur = [INF]*(n+1)
            hull_m = []  # slopes
            hull_b = []  # intercepts
            # We'll use lists and head index for deque to avoid import collections? Could use collections.deque.
            # Need add and popleft. Use collections.deque of tuples maybe fine. But for speed, use lists with head pointer.
            # Let's use lists of tuples? 1e6 operations, tuple overhead okay. But we can optimize.
            from collections import deque? We can import at top.
            hull = deque()
            kt = k*t
            for j in range(1, n+1):
                i = j-1
                if dp_prev[i] < INF//2:
                    m = -B[i]
                    b = dp_prev[i]
                    # add line to hull
                    # while len >=2 and bad(hull[-2], hull[-1], new)
                    while len(hull) >= 2:
                        m1,b1 = hull[-2]
                        m2,b2 = hull[-1]
                        # if (b2-b1)/(m1-m2) >= (b-b2)/(m2-m)
                        # m1 > m2 > m
                        if (b2 - b1) * (m2 - m) >= (b - b2) * (m1 - m2):
                            hull.pop()
                        else:
                            break
                    hull.append((m,b))
                if hull:
                    x = A[j] + kt
                    # pop front while second is no worse
                    while len(hull) >= 2:
                        m0,b0 = hull[0]
                        m1,b1 = hull[1]
                        if m0*x + b0 >= m1*x + b1:
                            hull.popleft()
                        else:
                            break
                    m0,b0 = hull[0]
                    dp_cur[j] = m0*x + b0 + x*B[j]
            ans = min(ans, dp_cur[n])
            dp_prev = dp_cur
        return ans

Let's write code carefully:

```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumCost(self, nums: List[int], cost: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)
        pref_num = [0]*(n+1)
        pref_cost = [0]*(n+1)
        for i in range(n):
            pref_num[i+1] = pref_num[i] + nums[i]
            pref_cost[i+1] = pref_cost[i] + cost[i]
        
        INF = 10**30
        dp_prev = [INF]*(n+1)
        dp_prev[0] = 0
        ans = INF
        
        for t in range(1, n+1):
            dp_cur = [INF]*(n+1)
            hull = deque()
            kt = k*t
            for j in range(1, n+1):
                i = j-1
                if dp_prev[i] < INF:
                    m = -pref_cost[i]
                    b = dp_prev[i]
                    while len(hull) >= 2:
                        m1, b1 = hull[-2]
                        m2, b2 = hull[-1]
                        if (b2 - b1) * (m2 - m) >= (b - b2) * (m1 - m2):
                            hull.pop()
                        else:
                            break
                    hull.append((m, b))
                
                if hull:
                    x = pref_num[j] + kt
                    while len(hull) >= 2:
                        m0, b0 = hull[0]
                        m1, b1 = hull[1]
                        if m0*x + b0 >= m1*x + b1:
                            hull.popleft()
                        else:
                            break
                    m0, b0 = hull[0]
                    dp_cur[j] = m0*x + b0 + x*pref_cost[j]
            if dp_cur[n] < ans:
                ans = dp_cur[n]
            dp_prev = dp_cur
        return ans
```

Let's maybe derive the bad condition one more time with actual code variables to avoid sign error.

We have lines:
l1 = (m1,b1), l2=(m2,b2), l3=(m,b) with m1 > m2 > m.
Intersection l1,l2: solve m1 x + b1 = m2 x + b2 -> x12 = (b2 - b1)/(m1 - m2). Since m1-m2 >0.
Intersection l2,l3: x23 = (b - b2)/(m2 - m). Since m2-m >0.
l2 is unnecessary if x12 >= x23.
So condition:
(b2 - b1)/(m1 - m2) >= (b - b2)/(m2 - m)
=> (b2 - b1)*(m2 - m) >= (b - b2)*(m1 - m2).
Code:
`if (b2 - b1) * (m2 - m) >= (b - b2) * (m1 - m2): hull.pop()`
Yes.

Now, let's test with l1 m=0,b=0; l2 m=-4,b=16; l3 m=-10,b=50:
x12=(16-0)/(0+4)=4; x23=(50-16)/(-4+10)=34/6=5.666; condition false. Code: (16)*(6)=96 >= (34)*(4)=136 false. Keep. Good.
If l2 useless l1 m=0,b=0; l2 m=-1,b=100; l3 m=-2,b=0:
x12=100; x23=(0-100)/(1)=-100; condition true. Code: (100)*(1)=100 >= (-100)*(1)=-100 true. Pop. Good.

Now, query while:
If hull [l1,l2] with m1>m2. l1 optimal for x < x12. At current x, if l1(x) >= l2(x), then x >= x12, so l1 never optimal for future. Pop. Code:
`if m0*x + b0 >= m1*x + b1: popleft()`
Yes.

But let's think more deeply: The insertion bad condition assumes the deque represents the lower envelope for all x, including x less than current. If we have already popped front lines in previous queries, the deque represents the lower envelope for x >= previous query. Is the insertion bad condition still valid for future queries if we don't have past lines? Yes, because we only care about future x. The bad condition with remaining lines computes the lower envelope for all x, but missing past lines could make a middle line appear useful when it's actually dominated by a past line for future? Wait, a past line has higher slope and is optimal for smaller x. Could a past line dominate a middle line for future x? If a past line has higher slope, for large x it becomes worse than lower slope lines. So if a line is dominated by a past line at some future x, it would be even more dominated by lower slope lines? Let's examine.

Suppose we popped l0 (past) because at current x, l1 (lower slope) was better. For future x, l1 remains better than l0. So l0 cannot dominate any line for future. Thus removing past lines doesn't affect future envelope. Good.

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumCost(self, nums: List[int], cost: List[int], k: int) -> int:
        n = len(nums)

        pref_num = [0] * (n + 1)
        pref_cost = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(n):
            pref_num[i + 1] = pref_num[i] + nums[i]
            pref_cost[i + 1] = pref_cost[i] + cost[i]

        INF = 10 ** 30
        dp_prev = [INF] * (n + 1)
        dp_prev[0] = 0
        ans = INF

        for t in range(1, n + 1):
            dp_cur = [INF] * (n + 1)
            hull = deque()
            kt = k * t

            for j in range(t, n + 1):
                i = j - 1
                if dp_prev[i] < INF:
                    m = -pref_cost[i]
                    b = dp_prev[i]

                    while len(hull) >= 2:
                        m1, b1 = hull[-2]
                        m2, b2 = hull[-1]
                        if (b2 - b1) * (m2 - m) >= (b - b2) * (m1 - m2):
                            hull.pop()
                        else:
                            break
                    hull.append((m, b))

                x = pref_num[j] + kt

                while len(hull) >= 2:
                    m0, b0 = hull[0]
                    m1, b1 = hull[1]
                    if m0 * x + b0 >= m1 * x + b1:
                        hull.popleft()
                    else:
                        break

                m0, b0 = hull[0]
                dp_cur[j] = m0 * x + b0 + x * pref_cost[j]

            ans = min(ans, dp_cur[n])
            dp_prev = dp_cur

        return ans
```
